# Audit: Silent Exception-Swallow Patterns — legacy/ADT module

*Read-only audit, 2026-07-06. Same method as `docs/AUDIT_Billing_exceptions.md` and `docs/AUDIT_Global_exceptions.md`; scope here is the ADT module (Admission-Discharge-Transfer, plus its ER/MRD/IPD sub-packages).*

**Scope:** every `.java` file under `legacy/ADT` (589 files, 2714 parsed catch blocks).

**Method:** read-only. Automated structural parse (comment/string-aware brace matching) of every `catch` block, classified by body content, cross-checked by manual sampling (spot-checked 15 findings and exclusions directly against source, across every severity category, both path classes, a multi-catch clause, and a rethrow exclusion — all matched their classification). No file under `legacy/` was modified.

**Definition used (per task, identical to the Billing/Global audits):** a finding is any catch block that is (a) empty, (b) returns `null`/a generic hardcoded error with no logging of the real cause, or (c) neither logs (via a real logger call) nor rethrows. Catch blocks that call a logger (`logger.error(...)`, etc.) or that `throw`/rethrow are excluded as non-findings.

## Summary

- **Total catch blocks parsed:** 2714
- **Total findings (silent-swallow pattern matches):** 2518
- **Non-findings:** 196 properly rethrow (`throw`); 0 properly log via a real logger call
- **By severity:** silent-swallow = 2474, status-masks-failure = 9, other = 35
- **By path:** billing-critical = 53, peripheral = 2465

| Severity | Billing-critical | Peripheral |
|---|---|---|
| status-masks-failure | 0 | 9 |
| silent-swallow | 53 | 2421 |
| other | 0 | 35 |

**0 of 589 files import any real logging library** (`org.apache.log4j.Logger` or `org.slf4j.Logger`) — worse than both prior audits (Billing 28/558, 5%; Global 15/2669, 0.6%). Every non-rethrowing catch in the module is therefore structurally silent by construction: there is no logging exclusion category available at all in this module (0 catches excluded on that basis, vs. 55 in Global and some in Billing).

## Methodology caveats (read before acting on this list)

- **billing-critical vs peripheral is a path/filename/import heuristic**, not a data-flow analysis, identical approach to the Global audit. A file is marked billing-critical if: (a) it lives under a `com/param/billing/` or `com/param/global/billing/` package (none do — ADT has no such package); (b) its path/filename contains a payment/invoice/tariff/discount/deposit/fund/insurance/razorpay/pesapal/charge/voucher/refund/bill/consumption/collection/sanction/mpesa/ccavenue keyword; or (c) it is one of the classes directly imported by `legacy/Billing` or `legacy/Global`. Criterion (c) contributes **zero** files here: the only ADT classes imported by Billing/Global are 34 `*.model.*` classes (e.g. `com.param.adt.admission.model.AdmissionDetails`), and **no `model` package exists anywhere in the current ADT source tree** — these are stale/broken imports referencing classes that do not exist in this baseline, the same class of pre-existing defect already logged as BR-002 for a different module. Criterion (b) is the only one that fired, on 53 findings across 4 files with actual catch blocks (`BillNoUtils.java`, `BillingBedCategoryMasterController.java`, `BillingBedCategoryMasterServiceImpl.java`, `BillingBedCategoryMasterDaoImpl.java`) — 9 further path-matched files (`IBillingBedCategoryMasterDao.java`, `IBillingBedCategoryMasterService.java`, `IPaymentTypeConstants.java`, `IBillingStatusConstants.java`, `PaymentEntitlementMasterDto.java`, `PaymentModeMasterDto.java`, `BillingBedVisitTypeMapperDto.java`, `UnitBillingBedCategoryMapperDto.java`, `BillingBedCategoryMasterDto.java`) are interfaces/DTOs/constants with no catch blocks at all, so they contribute 0 findings despite matching the path heuristic.
- **The `charge` keyword required a special case.** ADT is the Admission-Discharge-Transfer module, so "discharge" (patient discharge, a core domain term, ~500+ findings across the `discharge` package) appears constantly and is not a billing charge. A plain substring match on `charge` would have wrongly tagged the entire discharge-summary subsystem as billing-critical. The heuristic here excludes `charge` when it's part of `discharge` (regex `(?<!dis)charge`) so it still catches real billing terms like `surcharge`/`recharge` but not the ADT domain term — no such genuine `surcharge`/`recharge` occurrences exist in this module, so the net effect of the `charge` keyword in ADT is zero matches. Anyone re-running or extending this heuristic on other modules should re-check this special case still applies there.
- **The parser is structural, not control-flow-aware**, identical caveat to the Billing/Global audits: a catch block containing `throw` anywhere in its body is treated as "properly rethrows" and excluded, even if a nested conditional path could still silently fall through without throwing. Spot-checked directly against source (see Method) — confirmed correct on the samples checked, not exhaustively verified across all 196 excluded blocks.
- **`status-masks-failure`** is reserved for catches that `return null;` (and nothing else) — the exact BR-003/BR-005 pattern already fixed once in `GlobalExceptionHandler.java` (Spring turns a null return into an empty HTTP 200, i.e. failure looks like success). All 9 instances here are concentrated in a single file, `DischargeSummaryDaoImpl.java` (PDF-generation helper methods with `catch (FileNotFoundException|IOException|DocumentException e) { return null; }`).
- **`silent-swallow`** includes fully empty catch blocks and the common pattern of returning a generic/hardcoded error response. The per-row Notes column distinguishes: empty body; a return statement that never references the caught exception variable (the real cause is fully discarded); and a return statement (or no-op) where the exception variable is referenced somewhere in the body (e.g. assigned to an unused local) but the result is never logged or surfaced — same three-way split used in the Global audit.
- **`other`** is almost entirely `e.printStackTrace()`-only catches: visible on a console/stdout if anyone is watching it live, but not written to any persistent/centralized log, so effectively silent in a deployed environment.
- **Multi-catch clauses** (`catch (TypeA | TypeB e)`) are recorded once, under the last-listed type in the clause, as a parsing simplification — this undercounts distinct exception *types* seen but not the number of catch blocks/findings themselves. One such clause exists in this module (`DischargeSummaryDaoImpl.java:1885`, `DocumentException | IOException`), recorded under `IOException`.
- This audit does **not** re-scan `legacy/Billing` or `legacy/Global` (already catalogued in their own audit docs) or any other module — it is ADT-only, matching this task's scope.
